) and (
83
). The following corollary from
[
CJ16
]
expresses the integrals in
10.2
in terms of the
\omega
-coordinates.

Corollary 14.2

(Part of Corollary 2.4,
[
CJ16
]
)
.

For
n=4m
, let
x=(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathtt{W}_{\varepsilon_{1}}
and
y=(y_{1},y_{2})\in\mathtt{B}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
with
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in\{0,1\}
. Suppose that
-n<x_{1},x_{2},y_{1},y_{2}<0
,
\sqrt{2c}<p<1
and
Q^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}
is as given in (
137
). We have

(138)

\begin{split}~&\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(a,n+x_{1},n+x_{2},n+y_{1},n+y_{2})=\frac{\mathrm{i}^{\frac{x_{2}-x_{1}+y_{1}-y_{2}}{2}}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\int_{\Gamma_{p}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\omega_{1}}{\omega_{1}}\int_{\Gamma_{1/p}}\mathrm{d}\omega_{2}\\
&\times\frac{\omega_{2}}{\omega_{2}^{2}-\omega_{1}^{2}}\frac{H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{1})}{H_{y_{1},y_{2}+1}(\omega_{2})}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}=0}^{1}Q_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2}),\end{split}

Proof of
10.3
.

We have that (
88
) is
[
CJ16
, Equation (3.11)]
and we provide a short derivation.
The starting point for the formula for
K^{-1}_{a,1}
is given in
10.2
. Using
14.1
, we get (
87
) in
10.3
. We can use partial fractions on (
138
) and a change of variables to get (
88
), which was detailed in
[
CJ16
, Corollary 2.4 and Eq. (3.11)]
.
To get (
89
), we switch contours in (
88
) which picks up a single residue at
\omega_{2}=\omega_{1}
.
Equation

90
is given in
[
CJ16
, Lemma 3.3]
while the last statement is
[
CJ16
, Lemma 2.5]
.
∎

Proof of
10.4
.

This lemma follows from the formulas given in
[
CJ16
, Corollary 2.4]
and using the same steps used in the proof of
14.1
; see the proof of
[
CJ16
, Lemma 3.6]
.
∎

14.2.
Proof of
10.5

Here, we provide the proof of
10.5
. We split into the four cases given in the statement of the theorem. In each case, we ignore the integer parts as they are not important. Before the proof of each case, we provide the key lemmas and their proofs. We also need the following lemma, which is used for all the cases given in
10.5
.

Lemma 14.3
.

The behavior of
\mathrm{Im}\,g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega)
for
\omega\in\partial\mathbb{H}_{+}
is given by

(1)

\mathrm{Im}\,g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\mathrm{i}t)
decreases from
(1-2\xi_{1}+3\xi_{2})\frac{\pi}{2}
to
(1-\xi_{1}+3\xi_{2})\frac{\pi}{2}
for
t
increasing in
(0,\sqrt{2c})
,

(2)

\mathrm{Im}\,g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\mathrm{i}t)=(1-\xi_{1}+3\xi_{2})\frac{\pi}{2}
for
t\in(\sqrt{2c},1/\sqrt{2c})
,

(3)

\mathrm{Im}\,g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\mathrm{i}t)
increases from
(1-\xi_{1}+3\xi_{2})\frac{\pi}{2}
to
(1-\xi_{1}+2\xi_{2})\frac{\pi}{2}
for
t
increasing in
(1/\sqrt{2c},\infty)
,

(4)

\mathrm{Im}\,g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(t)=0
for
t\in(0,\infty)
,

(5)

\mathrm{Im}\,g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega)
increases from
0
to
(1-2\xi_{1}+3\xi_{2})\frac{\pi}{2}
depending on the angle of approach as
\omega
tends to 0,

(6)

\mathrm{Im}\,g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega)
decreases from
(1-\xi_{1}+2\xi_{2})\frac{\pi}{2}
to
0
depending on the angle of approach as
\omega
tends to infinity.

The proof of this lemma follows a very similar computation to
[
CJ16
, Lemma 3.8]
and so we omit the proof and refer the reader there.

Proof of (
103
) in

10.5
.

The case when
-\xi_{c}-\gamma<X,Y\leq-\xi_{c}+\gamma
(for
\gamma
sufficiently small) follows immediately due to the choices in contours for each of the four integrals involving
\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}
having the
\omega_{1}
-contour contained inside the
\omega_{2}
-contour and taking a simple bound. For the case when
m^{-1/6+\delta}\leq X,Y\leq-\xi_{c}-\gamma
we need the following lemma, which is proven after the proof of (
103
).

Lemma 14.4
.

For the scaling of
x
and
y
given in (
92
), we have that
\omega_{c,X}
and
\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}
defined through (
102
) are such that

\omega_{c,X},\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}\in\mathrm{i}(\sqrt{2c},1/\sqrt{2c})
. Then, we have that

•

\mathrm{Re}\,g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})<0
,

•

\mathrm{Re}\,g_{\tilde{\xi}_{1}+Y,\tilde{\xi}_{2}+Y}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})>0
,

•

g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})<0
,

•

g^{\prime\prime}_{\tilde{\xi}_{1}+Y,\tilde{\xi}_{2}+Y}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})>0
,

•

if
X=Y
and
(\tilde{\xi}_{1},\tilde{\xi}_{2})=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
, then
|\omega_{c,X}|<|\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}|
.

The curve of steepest descent of
\mathrm{Re}\,g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}
goes from
\omega_{c,X}
, perpendicular to the imaginary axis, to 0 and a curve of steepest ascent of
\mathrm{Re}\,g_{\tilde{\xi}_{1}+Y,\tilde{\xi}_{2}+Y}
from
\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}
, along the imaginary axis, to
\infty
.

We assume the above lemma and proceed with the proof of (
103
). We follow the saddle point analysis in
[
CJ16
, Proposition 3.12]
and refer the reader there for more details. Our starting point is the formula given in (
100
). We deform the contours to the curves given in
14.4
, splitting the integral into a local contribution around the saddle points
\omega_{c,X}
and
\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}
, and a remaining contribution which is exponentially small which can be ignored; see
[
CJ16
, Proposition 3.12]
. For the local contribution, we apply a change of variables
\omega_{1}=\omega_{c,X}+(2m)^{-1/2}w_{1}
and
\omega_{2}=\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}+(2m)^{-1/2}w_{2}
. Applying a Taylor expansion, the term in the first exponential in (
100
) becomes

\begin{split}&2mg_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{1})-\log G(\omega_{1})=2mg_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})-\log G(\omega_{c,X})\\
&+\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})w_{1}^{2}+O(w_{1}^{3}m^{-1/2})\\
&=\log H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})+\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})w_{1}^{2}+O(w_{1}^{3}m^{-1/2}).\end{split}

We have a similar formula in the
\omega_{2}
variable for the term in the second exponential in (
100
). Collecting the error terms in the above expansion means that (
100
) becomes

(139)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(a,n+x_{1},n+x_{2},n+y_{1},n+y_{2})\\
&=\frac{(1+O(m^{-1/2}))}{2m}\frac{\mathrm{i}^{\frac{x_{2}-x_{1}+y_{1}-y_{2}}{2}}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}w_{1}}{\omega_{c,X}}\int\mathrm{d}w_{2}\frac{H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})}{H_{y_{1},y_{2}+1}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})}\\
&\times\frac{V_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\omega_{c,X},\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})e^{\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})w_{1}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime}_{\tilde{\xi}_{1}+Y,\tilde{\xi}_{2}+Y}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})w_{2}^{2}}}{\omega_{c,X}-\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}+(2m)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(w_{1}-w_{2})}\end{split}

where the contours are local contours around the origin (of length
m^{-\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon>0
) with the
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
contours are positively oriented along the real and imaginary axis respectively, which follows from
14.4
. If
\omega_{c,X}=\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}
, the above singularity is integrable. In any case and noting the signs in the exponent from
14.4
, we extend the contours to infinity which gives a Gaussian integral and so we obtain a bound

|\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(a,n+x_{1},n+x_{2},n+y_{1},n+y_{2})|\leq\frac{\mathtt{C}_{1}}{\sqrt{m}}\bigg|\frac{H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})}{H_{y_{1},y_{2}+1}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})}\bigg|

The result then follows by using the exponential form of
H
given in (
97
) and third and fourth statements in
14.4
, and noting that
2m\mathrm{Re}\,g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})
is at most
-m^{5/6+\delta}
from the choice of scaling in (
92
), and that
V
is uniformly bounded on compact subsets.

It remains to prove the assumed lemma stated above.

Proof of
14.4
.

The proof of this lemma uses the following two lemmas from
[
CJ16
]
.

Lemma 14.5

(Lemma 3.9 in
[
CJ16
]
)
.

The function
g_{\xi,\xi}(\omega)
for
\omega\in\mathbb{H}_{+}\cup\partial\mathbb{H}_{+}
has two distinct saddle points in the interval
(\sqrt{2c},1/\sqrt{2c})\mathrm{i}
(but not equal to
\mathrm{i}
) if and only if
-1/2\sqrt{1-2c}<\xi<0
. These saddle points are given by
\omega_{c}\in(\sqrt{2c},1)\mathrm{i}
and
-\omega_{c}^{-1}
. We also have
\mathrm{Re}\,g_{\xi,\xi}(\omega_{c})<0
and
\mathrm{Re}\,g_{\xi,\xi}(-\omega_{c}^{-1})>0
.

Lemma 14.6

(Lemma 3.10 in
[
CJ16
]
)
.

For
-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{1-2c}<\xi<0
, choose
\omega_{c}
such that
g_{\xi,\xi}^{\prime}(\omega_{c})=0
with
\omega_{c}\in(\sqrt{2c},1)\mathrm{i}
. Then
g_{\xi,\xi}^{\prime}(-\omega_{c}^{-1})=0
,

g_{\xi,\xi}^{\prime\prime}(\omega_{c})<0\hskip 14.22636pt\mbox{and}\hskip 14.22636ptg_{\xi,\xi}^{\prime\prime}(-\omega_{c}^{-1})>0.

Turning to the proof of
14.4
, letting
\omega=\mathrm{i}t
and recalling
\theta
defined in (
78
), we have that

0=\mathrm{i}tg^{\prime}_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\mathrm{i}t)=1+(\xi_{1}+X)\theta(t)+(\xi_{2}+X)\theta(t^{-1}).

When
\xi_{1}=\xi_{2}
, the right-hand side above has zeroes at points
t\in(\sqrt{2c},1)
and
t^{-1}
; this is part of
14.5
. Since the function is continuous in
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}
, these two zeroes will move continuously as we vary these parameters in an
O(m^{-1/6})
window. This produces the two critical points defined in (
102
), and verifies the fifth bullet point.

Next, using the above two lemmas and the fact that
|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}|<m^{-\frac{1}{6}}
,
g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}
is analytic in
\omega
and locally smooth as a function of
\omega,\xi_{1},\xi_{2}
, we get the first four bullet points.

Finally, since
g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\backslash(\mathrm{i}(-\infty,-1/\sqrt{2c}]\cup\mathrm{i}[-\sqrt{2c},\sqrt{2c}]\cup\mathrm{i}[1/\sqrt{2c},\infty))
the contours of steepest descent of
\mathrm{Re}\,g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}
are the level lines of
\mathrm{Im}\,g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}
.
14.3
gives that
\mathrm{Im}\,g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})=\frac{\pi}{2}(1-\xi_{1}+3\xi_{2}+2X)
and statement 3 of this lemma means that the descent contour goes perpendicular to the imaginary axis and either ends at 0 or
\infty
by the fifth and sixth statements in
14.3
. We have that the steepest ascent contour of
\mathrm{Re}\,g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}
must go along the imaginary axis, by the fourth statement of this lemma, to either 0 or
\infty
by the fifth and sixth statements of
14.3
. Since the ascent and descent contours cannot cross and by the ordering given in the fifth statement of this lemma, we have that the descent contour must end at 0, while the ascent contour ends at
\infty
.
∎

∎

Proof of (
104
) in
10.5
.

We follow the same proof as given for (
103
) but the analysis needs to be slightly refined and we need the following lemma which is proved below.

Lemma 14.7
.

We have that
\omega_{c,X}
and
\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}
satisfy the statements of
14.4
along with
g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})=O(X)
and
g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1}+Y,\xi_{2}+Y}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})=O(Y)
. We have that
|\omega_{c,0}|>|\omega_{c,X}|
and
\omega_{c,0}-\omega_{c,X}=O(\sqrt{X})
and

|\tilde{\omega}_{c,0}|<|\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}|
and
\tilde{\omega}_{c,0}-\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}=O(\sqrt{Y})
.

As in the proof of (
103
), we start with (
100
) and obtain (
139
) with the contours for the
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
as stated there. As given in the proof (
103
) we compute the Gaussian integrals and we obtain the bound

(140)

\begin{split}&|\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(a,n+x_{1},n+x_{2},n+y_{1},n+y_{2})\leq\bigg|\frac{H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})}{H_{y_{1},y_{2}+1}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})}\bigg|\\
&\times\frac{\mathtt{C}_{1}}{\sqrt{-mg^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+Y}(\omega_{c,X})g^{\prime\prime}_{\tilde{\xi}_{1}+X,\tilde{\xi}_{2}+X}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})}}\end{split}

where
\mathtt{C}_{1}
is a positive constant.
In the above equation, it suffices to estimate
H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})
as the estimate for
H_{y_{1},y_{2}+1}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})
follows by a similar computation. Taking the logarithms and using (
97
), we need to consider

\begin{split}&g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})=g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})-X(\log G(\omega_{c,X})-\log G(\omega_{c,X}^{-1}))\\
&=g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{c,0})-X(\log G(\omega_{c,0})-\log G(\omega_{c,0}^{-1}))\\
&+(\omega_{c,X}-\omega_{c,0})\frac{X}{\mathrm{i}t_{c,0}}(\theta(t_{c,0})+\theta(t_{c,0}^{-1}))+O(X(\omega_{c,X}-\omega_{c,0})^{2})\\
&=g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,0})+(t_{c,X}-t_{c,0})\frac{X}{t_{c,0}}(\theta(t_{c,0})+\theta(t_{c,0}^{-1}))+O(X(\omega_{c,X}-\omega_{c,0})^{2})\end{split}

where the second line follows by a Taylor series, setting
\omega_{c,0}=\mathrm{i}t_{c,0}
and
\omega_{c,X}=\mathrm{i}t_{c,X}
, using that
\omega_{c,0}
satisfies
g^{\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{c,0})=g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{c,0})=0
, and using the definition of
\theta
in (
78
). We then have

|H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})|=|H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,0})|e^{(t_{c,X}-t_{c,0})\frac{X}{t_{c,0}}(\theta(t_{c,0})+\theta(t_{c,0}^{-1}))+O(X^{2})}.

The result follows from using the bound on
(\omega_{c,X}-\omega_{c,0})
in
14.7
, applying the same type of computation to
{H_{y_{1},y_{2}+1}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})}
and subsituting the estimates back into (
140
).

Proof of
14.7
.

We prove the lemma for
X
; the assertions hold for
Y
by similar computations.
Since
(\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X)
is not on the curve (
144
), we have that that
g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,X})\not=0
and so
\omega_{c,X}
satisfy the statements of
14.4
by analyticity.

Let
\omega_{c,0}=\mathrm{i}t_{c,0}
,
\omega_{c,X}=\mathrm{i}t_{c,X}
and recall (
78
).
Suppose that
t_{c,X}-t_{c,0}=\epsilon
. From (
101
) with
\xi_{i}\mapsto\xi_{i}+X
for
i\in\{1,2\}
, we get from a Taylor expansion

(141)

\begin{split}0&=1+(\xi_{1}+X)\theta(t_{c,X})+(\xi_{2}+X)\theta((t_{c,X})^{-1})\\
&=1+\xi_{1}\theta(t_{c,0})+\xi_{2}\theta(t_{c,0}^{-1})+\epsilon\big(\xi_{1}\theta^{\prime}(t_{c,0})+\xi_{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\theta(t^{-1})\big|_{t=t_{c,0}}\big)\\
&+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\big(\xi_{1}\theta^{\prime\prime}(t_{c,0})+\xi_{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}\theta(t^{-1})\big|_{t=t_{c,0}}\big)+X(\theta(t_{c,X})+\theta(t_{c,X}^{-1}))+O(\epsilon^{3})\\
&=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\big(\xi_{1}\theta^{\prime\prime}(t_{c,0})+\xi_{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}\theta(t^{-1})\big|_{t=t_{c,0}}\big)+X(\theta(t_{c,X})+\theta(t_{c,X}^{-1}))+O(\epsilon^{3})\end{split}

where the last line follows since
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
is a double zero of (
101
). The remaining terms in the above equation are non-zero and noting that
\theta^{\prime\prime}(t_{c,0}),\theta(t_{c,0})>0
and
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}<0
it follows
\epsilon
is real and is
O(\sqrt{X})
. Finally, we get
|\omega_{c,0}|>|\omega_{c,X}|
which holds by the fifth statement of
14.4
and using analyticity.
k ∎

Proof of (
105
) in
10.5
.

We need the following lemma which we prove first before commencing with the proof of (
105
).

Lemma 14.8
.

For
\omega_{c,0}=\mathrm{i}t_{c,0}
, we have that
t_{c,0}\in(\sqrt{2c},1/\sqrt{2c})
and that

g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\mathrm{i}t_{c,0})=-\mathrm{i}\mathcal{G}_{c,t}

with
\omega_{c,0}=\mathrm{i}t_{c,0}
and
t\in(\sqrt{2c},1/\sqrt{2c})
where

\mathcal{G}_{c,t}=\frac{2c(2c-t^{2}+8c^{2}t^{2}-4ct^{4}-3t^{6}+6ct^{8})}{t^{5}(2c-t^{2})(-1+2ct^{2})(-1+4ct^{2}-t^{4})}.

In
\mathbb{H}_{+}\cup\partial\mathbb{H}_{+}
, there is a curve of steepest descent leaving
\omega_{c,0}
at angle
-\pi/6
going to the origin and a curve of steepest ascent leaving
\omega_{c,0}
at angle
\pi/6
going to
\infty
.

Proof of
14.8
.

We can differentiate (
101
) which gives

(142)

\omega g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega)+g^{\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega)=\xi_{1}\frac{2c}{(\omega^{2}+2c)^{\frac{3}{2}}}-\xi_{2}\frac{2c}{(\omega^{-2}+2c)^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

Asserting that
g^{\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{c,0})=g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{c,0})=0
in (
101
) and the above equation gives a matrix equation for
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
which can be solved giving

(143)

\begin{split}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})&=\bigg(\frac{-\omega_{c,0}(\omega_{c,0}^{2}+2c)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{1+4c\omega_{c,0}^{2}+\omega_{c,0}^{4}},\frac{-(\omega_{c,0}^{-2}+2c)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\omega_{c,0}(1+4c\omega_{c,0}^{2}+\omega_{c,0}^{4})}\bigg)\\
&=\bigg(\frac{-t_{c,0}(t_{c,0}^{2}-2c)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{1-4ct_{c,0}^{2}+t_{c,0}^{4}},\frac{-(t_{c,0}^{-2}-2c)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{t_{c,0}(1-4ct_{c,0}^{2}+t_{c,0}^{4})}\bigg)\end{split}

where
\omega_{c,0}=\mathrm{i}t_{c,0}
. The above equation solves (
144
) and we need to verify that it is on the rough-smooth boundary. To see this, notice that for
t_{c,0}\in(\sqrt{2c},1/\sqrt{2c})
, we have that
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}<0
and for
t_{c,0}=1
,
\xi_{1}=\xi_{2}=\xi_{c}
as needed.

We differentiate (
142
) which gives

\omega g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega)+2g^{\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega)=-\frac{6c\omega}{(\omega^{2}+2c)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\xi_{1}+\frac{2c(-1+4c\omega^{2})}{\sqrt{2c+\omega^{-2}}(\omega+2c\omega^{3})^{2}}\xi_{2}.

This gives

g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\mathrm{i}t_{c,0})=-\frac{6c}{\mathrm{i}(t_{c,0}^{2}-2c)^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\frac{2c(-1-4ct_{c,0}^{2})}{\mathrm{i}t_{c,0}\sqrt{2c+t_{c,0}^{-2}}(t_{c,0}-2ct_{c,0}^{3})^{2}}\xi_{2}.

Substituting the values of
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
found in (
143
) gives the result for
g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}
after a computation.

The local angles of the curves of steepest descent and ascent around the double critical point follows from the fact that
\mathcal{G}_{c,t}>0
for
t\in(\sqrt{2c},1/\sqrt{2c})
. The rest of the argument for the location of the curves of steepest descent and ascent is the same as the one in
14.4
. ∎

We now proceed with the proof of (
105
) in
10.5
.
We follow the saddle point analysis in
[
CJ16
, Proposition 3.17]
and refer the reader there for more details.
Our starting point is the formula given in (
100
). We deform the contours to those given in
14.8
, splitting the integral to a local contribution around the saddle points
\omega_{c,0}
and
\tilde{\omega}_{c,0}
defined through (
102
), and a remaining contribution which is exponentially small which can be ignored; see
[
CJ16
, Proposition 3.17]
. For the local contribution, we apply a change of variables
\omega_{1}=\omega_{c,0}+(2m)^{-1/3}w_{1}
and
\omega_{2}=\tilde{\omega}_{c,0}+(2m)^{-1/3}w_{2}
. Applying a Taylor series gives the terms in the exponent with respect to
\omega_{1}
becoming

\begin{split}&2mg_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{1})-\log G(\omega_{1})\\
&=2mg_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{1})-(2mX+1)\log G(\omega_{1})+2mX\log G(\omega_{1}^{-1})\\
&=2mg_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{c,0})-\log G(\omega_{c,0})\\
&+\frac{1}{6}g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{c,0})w_{1}^{3}+(2m)^{\frac{2}{3}}Xw_{1}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\omega_{c,0}^{2}+2c}}+\frac{1}{\omega_{c,0}^{2}\sqrt{\omega_{c,0}^{-2}+2c}}\bigg)\\
&+O(w_{1}^{3}m^{-1/3})=\log H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,0})+\frac{1}{6}g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{c,0})w_{1}^{2}\\
&+\frac{(2m)^{\frac{2}{3}}Xw_{1}}{\omega_{c,0}}(\theta(-\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,0})-\theta(\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,0}^{-1}))+O(w_{1}^{3}m^{-1/3}).\end{split}

In the above display, we have rewrite
g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega_{1})
in terms of
g_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{1})
and an additional term from (
99
) in order to facilitate the Taylor series. We have also used
-\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,0}\in(\sqrt{2c},1/\sqrt{2c})
to keep the notation compact.
A similar expression holds for the
\omega_{2}
variable. Collecting the error terms and the above expansion means that (
100
) becomes

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(a,n+x_{1},n+x_{2},n+y_{1},n+y_{2})=\frac{(1+O(m^{-1/3}))}{(2m)^{2/3}}\frac{\mathrm{i}^{\frac{x_{2}-x_{1}+y_{1}-y_{2}}{2}}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\\
&\times\int\frac{\mathrm{d}w_{1}}{\omega_{c,X}}\int\mathrm{d}w_{2}\frac{V_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\omega_{c,0},\tilde{\omega}_{c,0})e^{\frac{1}{6}g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}(\omega_{c,0})w_{1}^{3}-\frac{1}{6}g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\tilde{\xi}_{1},\tilde{\xi}_{2}}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,0})w_{2}^{3}}}{\omega_{c,0}-\tilde{\omega}_{c,0}+(2m)^{-\frac{1}{3}}(w_{1}-w_{2})}\frac{H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})}{H_{y_{1},y_{2}+1}(\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y})}\\
&\times e^{\frac{(2m)^{\frac{2}{3}}Xw_{1}}{\omega_{c,0}}(\theta(-\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,0})-\theta(\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,0}^{-1}))-\frac{(2m)^{\frac{2}{3}}Yw_{2}}{\tilde{\omega}_{c,0}}(\theta(-\mathrm{i}\tilde{\omega}_{c,0})-\theta(\mathrm{i}\tilde{\omega}_{c,0}^{-1}))}\end{split}

where the contours are local contours around the origin (of length
m^{-\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon>0
) with the
w_{1}
contour leaving the origin at
-\pi/6
and
w_{2}
contour leaving the origin at
\pi/6
, which follows from
14.8
. From the choice
|\xi_{1}-\tilde{\xi}_{1}|<m^{-1/3}
, we have that
\omega_{c,0}=\tilde{\omega}_{c,0}
up to an error of
O(m^{-1/3})
by using (
143
) to estimate
|\omega_{c,0}-\tilde{\omega}_{c,0}|
from
|\xi_{1}-\tilde{\xi}_{1}|
. Note that the above singularity is integrable. We take the change of variables
w_{i}\mapsto\mathrm{i}w_{i}
, extend the contours to infinity and so the above formula is equal to the Airy kernel evaluated at

\bigg(\frac{(2m)^{\frac{2}{3}}X}{\omega_{c,0}}(\theta(-\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,0})-\theta(\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,0}^{-1})),\frac{(2m)^{\frac{2}{3}}Y}{{\omega}_{c,0}}(\theta(-\mathrm{i}{\omega}_{c,0})-\theta(\mathrm{i}{\omega}_{c,0}^{-1}))\bigg)

which is bounded due to the choice of
X
and
Y
in (
94
). The result follows.

Proof of (
106
) in
10.5
.

We give a shortened proof of this result since we mirror the proof of
[
CJ16
, Section 3.6]
.
We need to find the asymptotic behaviour of (
100
) and so we first analyze (
101
) to understand the roots of this equation. These roots will enable us to determine the choice of contours in (
100
).

It is sufficient to analyze (
101
) by a Taylor series.
For
\omega_{c,X}
defined by (
102
), we have that
\overline{\omega_{c,X}}
,
-\omega_{c,X}
and
-\overline{\omega_{c,X}}
are also roots of
g^{\prime}_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}(\omega)=0
. We next determine that
\omega_{c,X}\in\mathbb{H}_{+}
for the choice of
X
given in (
95
) since if
\omega_{c,X}\in\mathbb{H}_{+}
, then these four points are all distinct and define four roots of (
101
) —
[
CJ16
, Lemma 3.7]
states there are at most four roots of (
101
). By a Taylor series (cf. with the computation for (
141
)), we get that

0=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}(\xi_{1}\theta^{\prime\prime}(-\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,0})+\xi_{2}\theta^{\prime\prime}(\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,0}^{-1}))+X(\theta(-\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,X})+\theta(\mathrm{i}\omega_{c,X}^{-1}))+O(\epsilon^{3})

where
\omega_{c,X}=\omega_{c,0}+\epsilon
. Since
X<0,\theta(t)>0,\theta^{\prime\prime}(t)<0
for
t\in(\sqrt{2c},1/\sqrt{2c})

\xi_{1},\xi_{2}<0
we have that
\epsilon
is
O(\sqrt{|X|})
and real and so
\omega_{c,X}\in\mathbb{H}_{+}
. Moreover, we can write
\omega_{c,X}=r_{c,X}e^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{c,X}}
with
r_{c,X}>0
and
0<\theta_{c,X}<\pi/2
. Since both
|X-Y|<Cm^{-1}
and
|\xi_{1}-\tilde{\xi}_{1}|<Cm^{-1}
for some
C>1
, we have that
\omega_{c,X}=\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}
because
g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}
and
g_{\tilde{\xi}_{1}+Y,\tilde{\xi}_{2}+Y}
differ by
O(m^{-1})
.

We now choose the contours for the integral in (
100
) so that they pass through the four roots
\omega_{c,X},-\omega_{c,X},\overline{\omega_{c,X}}
and
-\overline{\omega_{c,X}}
and are given by the curves of steepest ascent and descent for
\mathrm{Re}g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}
. By analyticity and since
|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}|<m^{-\frac{1}{6}}
, we get similar steepest descent and ascent curves for
\mathrm{Re}g_{\xi_{1}+X,\xi_{2}+X}
as given in
[
CJ16
, Lemma 3.20]
, namely

•

a contour of steepest ascent leaving
\omega_{c,X}
at an angle
\theta_{c,X}-\pi/4
ending at infinity,

•

a contour of steepest descent leaving
\omega_{c,X}
at an angle
\theta_{c,X}+\pi/4
ending at a cut and a descent
contour ending at
\mathrm{i}/\sqrt{2c}
traveling via the cut
\mathrm{i}[1/\sqrt{2c},\infty)
,

•

a contour of steepest ascent leaving
\omega_{c,X}
at an angle
\theta_{c,X}+3\pi/4
ending at a cut and an ascent
contour ending at
\mathrm{i}/\sqrt{2c}
traveling via the cut
\mathrm{i}[0,\sqrt{2c}]
, and

•

a contour of steepest descent leaving
\omega_{c,X}
at an angle
\theta_{c,X}-3\pi/4
ending at zero.

see
[
CJ16
, Lemma 3.20]
for full details on obtaining these contours. Deforming the contours of (
100
) to these curves of steepest descent and ascent means that the contours cross, which picks up a single integral term,
C_{\omega_{c,X}}(x,y)
while the double integral term is
O(m^{-1/2})
which follows readily by standard saddle point arguments in a rough region. The length of the contour for
C_{\omega_{c,X}}(x,y)
,
|\Gamma_{\omega_{c,X}}|
, is
2|-\overline{\omega_{c,X}}-\omega_{c,X}|
which is order
\sqrt{|X|}
as shown above. Since the integrand for
C_{\omega_{c,X}}(x,y)
is bounded away from zero, we get that
|C_{\omega_{c,X}}(x,y)|
is order
\sqrt{|X|}
as required.
∎

14.3.
Proof of
10.6

Here, we give the proof of
10.6
.

Proof.

The first equation is simply a restatement of the first equation in
10.3
. The second equation, holds for
-C<\alpha_{x},\alpha_{y}<C
by
[
CJ16
, Theorem 2.7]
. To extend the range, the same proof as the one given in
[
CJ16
, Proposition 3.4]
holds; see
[
CJ16
, Proposition 3.4]
for further details as we omit this computation.

The final equation holds for
-C<\alpha_{x},\alpha_{y}<C
by
[
CJ16
, Theorem 2.7]
. We need to check the equation when either
C<\alpha_{x}<\log^{3}m
or
C<\alpha_{y}<\log^{3}m
(or both), that is, we need to check that the asymptotics has the same decay as the one for the extended Airy kernel. We use a refined asymptotic analysis of the one used to prove (
104
), checking only the case
C<\alpha_{x}<\log^{3}m
as the others are similar.

We start with (
88
). We note that with the scaling given by (
96
) with
f_{x}=(1,2\varepsilon)
, we have after expanding out that

\begin{split}&\log H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{1})=2mg_{\xi_{c}+X,\xi_{c}+X}(\omega_{1})-\log G(\omega_{1})\\
&-\big(2\lfloor\hat{\beta}_{x}\lambda_{2}(2m)^{\frac{2}{3}}+k_{x}\lambda_{2}\log^{2}m\rfloor\big)\big(\log G(\omega_{1})+\log(G(\omega_{1}^{-1}))\big)\end{split}

where
X=\frac{1}{2(2m)}\lfloor\alpha_{x}\lambda_{1}(2m)^{1/3}\rfloor
and

g_{\xi_{c}+X,\xi_{c}+X}(\omega_{1})=g_{\xi_{c},\xi_{c}}(\omega_{1})-X\log G(\omega_{1})+X\log G(\omega_{1}^{-1}).

The
\omega_{2}
integral has the exact same computation as the one given in
[
CJ16
, Proposition 3.17]
so we primarily focus on
\omega_{1}
terms.

We have that the critical point of
g_{\xi_{c}+X,\xi_{c}+X}(\omega_{1})
is given by
\omega_{c,X}
where
\omega_{c,X}
satifies the statements given in
14.4
and
14.7
. We deform the contour with respect to
\omega_{1}
to the steepest desecent contour given in
14.4
while we deform the contour with respect to
\omega_{2}
to the steepest ascent contour given in
[
CJ16
, Lemma 3.15]
.

We next split the integral to a local contribution around the saddle points
\omega_{c,X}
and
\tilde{\omega}_{c,Y}
, determined in (
14.4
), and a remaining contribution which is exponentially small which can be ignored; see
[
CJ16
, Proposition 3.12]
. For the local contribution, we apply a change of variables
\omega_{1}=\omega_{c,X}+(2m)^{-1/3}c_{0}w
and
\omega_{2}=\mathrm{i}+(2m)^{-1/3}c_{0}z
where
|w|,|z|<m^{\delta}
for
\delta>0
. We get from a Taylor series

\begin{split}&\frac{H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{1})}{H_{y_{1},y_{2}+1}(\omega_{2})}=\frac{H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})}{G(\mathrm{i}^{-1})^{\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}+1}{2}}}\exp\Bigg(g_{\xi_{c}+X,\xi_{c}+X}^{\prime\prime}(\omega_{c,X})c_{0}^{2}w^{2}\\
&-2\lfloor\hat{\beta}_{x}\lambda_{2}(2m)^{\frac{1}{6}}\rfloor\bigg(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\omega_{c,X}^{2}+2c}}+\frac{1}{\omega_{c,X}^{2}\sqrt{\omega_{c,X}^{-2}+2c}}\bigg)c_{0}w\\
&+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{3}{z^{3}}+\hat{\beta}_{y}z^{2}-\mathrm{i}\alpha_{y}z+\mathrm{Err}\Bigg)\end{split}

where
\mathrm{Err}
is equal to
m^{-1/2}w^{3}R_{m}(w)+m^{-1/3}z^{4}S_{m}(z)
and both
R_{m}(w)
and
S_{m}(z)
tend to constants as
m
tends to infinity for
|z|,|w|<m^{\delta}
. We also have that

\frac{V_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})}{\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}}=\frac{V_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\omega_{c,X},\mathrm{i})}{\mathrm{i}-\omega_{c,X}}+O(m^{-\frac{1}{3}}).

Substituting both of the two expansions back into (
88
), along with the local change of variables, after extending the contours to infinity, we compute the Airy and Gaussian integrals with respect to
z
and
w
respectively. We have that
\omega_{c,X}=\mathrm{i}+c_{2}\mathrm{i}\sqrt{X}
from
14.7
. In fact, from (
141
) with
\xi_{1}=\xi_{2}=\xi_{c}
,
t_{c,0}=1
,
t_{c,X}=c_{2}\sqrt{X}
in that equation, we get that
c_{2}=-\frac{(1-2c)^{3/4}}{\sqrt{c(1+c)}}
. Applying another Taylor series and computing the relevant derivatives gives

\begin{split}&H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})=H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\mathrm{i})\exp\Bigg(-2m\frac{2\mathrm{i}c(1+c)}{3(1-2c)^{2}}(\mathrm{i}c_{2}\sqrt{X})^{3}\\
&-\frac{2\mathrm{i}\alpha_{x}m^{1/3}\lambda_{1}}{\sqrt{1-2c}}(\mathrm{i}c_{2}\sqrt{X})-\frac{2c\hat{\beta}_{x}\lambda_{2}m^{2/3}}{(1-2c)^{3/2}}(\mathrm{i}c_{2}\sqrt{X})^{2}+o(1)\Bigg)\end{split}

as
m\to\infty
. Plugging in the constants for
\lambda_{1}
,
\lambda_{2}
and
c_{2}
gives

H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\omega_{c,X})=H_{x_{1}+1,x_{2}}(\mathrm{i})\exp\big(-\frac{2}{3}\alpha_{x}^{3/2}+\hat{\beta}_{x}\alpha_{x}\big).

We also have the coefficient term of

\begin{split}&\frac{V_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\omega_{c,X},\mathrm{i})}{\mathrm{i}-\omega_{c,X}}\frac{c_{0}m^{-5/6}}{(2\pi)^{3/2}\sqrt{-g_{\xi_{c}+X,\xi_{c}+X}^{\prime\prime}(\omega_{c,X})}}\\
&=\frac{V_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathrm{i},\mathrm{i})}{-c_{2}\mathrm{i}\sqrt{X}}\frac{c_{0}m^{-5/6}}{(2\pi)^{3/2}\sqrt{\frac{4c(c+1)}{(1-2c)^{2}}c_{2}}X^{1/4}}+o(1)\\
&=\frac{V_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(\mathrm{i},\mathrm{i})c_{0}m^{-1/3}2^{3/4}}{-c_{2}\mathrm{i}(2\pi)^{3/2}\sqrt{\frac{4c(c+1)}{(1-2c)^{2}}c_{2}}\alpha^{3/4}\lambda_{1}^{3/4}}+o(1)\\
\end{split}

Multiplying the above two equations, expanding out the constants and multiplying the omitted coefficients gives the same decay and coefficients as the third equation of
10.6
when
C<\alpha_{x}<\log^{3}m
as required.
∎

Appendix A
Limit Shape Curve

The limit shape curve
\xi
of the two-periodic Aztec diamond with corners
(-1,-1)
,
(1,-1)
,
(1,1)
and
(-1,1)
with
c=a/(1+a^{2})
is given by the equation

(144)

\begin{split}&64c^{6}\left(-1+\xi_{1}^{2}\right)\left(-1+\xi_{2}^{2}\right)-\left(\xi_{1}^{4}+\left(-1+\xi_{2}^{2}\right){}^{2}-2\xi_{1}^{2}\left(1+\xi_{2}^{2}\right)\right){}^{2}\\
&-16c^{4}\left(3\left(-1+\xi_{2}^{2}\right){}^{2}+\xi_{1}^{2}\left(-6+27\xi_{2}^{2}-20\xi_{2}^{4}\right)+\xi_{1}^{4}\left(3-20\xi_{2}^{2}+16\xi_{2}^{4}\right)\right)\\
&-4c^{2}\left(3\left(-1+\xi_{2}^{2}\right){}^{3}+\xi_{1}^{6}\left(3+8\xi_{2}^{2}\right)+\xi_{1}^{4}\left(-9+13\xi_{2}^{2}-16\xi_{2}^{4}\right)\right.\\
&\left.+\xi_{1}^{2}\left(9-30\xi_{2}^{2}+13\xi_{2}^{4}+8\xi_{2}^{6}\right)\right)=0.\end{split}

The set of solutions of the above equation has two connected components, which are given by two simple closed curves surrounding the origin. The inner of these two curves is the
rough-smooth limit curve
, and the out curve is the
frozen-rough limit curve
. Only the rough-smooth limit curve is relevant in our setting.

Appendix B
The formula for
Y_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}

Let

\begin{split}f_{a,b}(u,v)=&\left(2a^{2}uv+2b^{2}uv-ab\left(-1+u^{2}\right)\left(-1+v^{2}\right)\right)\\
&\times\left(2a^{2}uv+2b^{2}uv+ab\left(-1+u^{2}\right)\left(-1+v^{2}\right)\right).\end{split}

Define the following rational functions:

\displaystyle\mathtt{y}_{0,0}^{0,0}(a,b,u,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\left(a^{2}+b^{2}\right)^{2}f_{a,b}(u,v)}

\displaystyle\times\Big(2a^{7}u^{2}v^{2}-a^{5}b^{2}\left(1+u^{4}+u^{2}v^{2}-u^{4}v^{2}+v^{4}-u^{2}v^{4}\right)

\displaystyle-a^{3}b^{4}\left(1+3u^{2}+3v^{2}+2u^{2}v^{2}+u^{4}v^{2}+u^{2}v^{4}-u^{4}v^{4}\right)

\displaystyle-ab^{6}\left(1+v^{2}+u^{2}+3u^{2}v^{2}\right)\Big),

\displaystyle\mathtt{y}_{0,1}^{0,0}(a,b,u,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{a\left(b^{2}+a^{2}u^{2}\right)\left(2a^{2}v^{2}+b^{2}\left(1+v^{2}-u^{2}+u^{2}v^{2}\right)\right)}{4\left(a^{2}+b^{2}\right)f_{a,b}(u,v)},

\displaystyle\mathtt{y}_{1,0}^{0,0}(a,b,u,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{a\left(b^{2}+a^{2}v^{2}\right)\left(2a^{2}u^{2}+b^{2}\left(1-v^{2}+u^{2}+u^{2}v^{2}\right)\right)}{4\left(a^{2}+b^{2}\right)f_{a,b}(u,v)},

\displaystyle\mathtt{y}_{1,1}^{0,0}(a,b,u,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{a\left(2a^{2}u^{2}v^{2}+b^{2}\left(-1+v^{2}+u^{2}+u^{2}v^{2}\right)\right)}{4f_{a,b}(u,v)}.

For
i,j\in\{0,1\}
, define
\mathtt{y}^{0,1}_{i,j}(a,b,u,v)
,
\mathtt{y}^{1,0}_{i,j}(a,b,u,v)
and
\mathtt{y}^{1,1}_{i,j}(a,b,u,v)
by

\begin{split}\mathtt{y}^{0,1}_{i,j}(a,b,u,v)&=\frac{\mathtt{y}^{0,0}_{i,j}(b,a,u,v^{-1})}{v^{2}},\\
\mathtt{y}^{1,0}_{i,j}(a,b,u,v)&=\frac{\mathtt{y}^{0,0}_{i,j}(b,a,u^{-1},v)}{u^{2}},\\
\mathtt{y}^{1,1}_{i,j}(a,b,u,v)&=\frac{\mathtt{y}^{0,0}_{i,j}(a,b,u^{-1},v^{-1})}{u^{2}v^{2}}.\end{split}

When
b=1
, we write
y^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}_{i,j}(a,1,u,v)=y^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}_{i,j}(u,v)
.
We then have

(145)

\begin{split}&Y^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}(u_{1},u_{2})=Y^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}(a,u_{1},u_{2})=(1+a^{2})^{2}\\
&\times(-1)^{\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}+\gamma_{1}(1+\varepsilon_{2})+\gamma_{2}(1+\varepsilon_{1})}s(-\mathrm{i}u_{1})^{\gamma_{1}}s(-\mathrm{i}u_{2})^{\gamma_{2}}\mathtt{y}_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}^{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}(-\mathrm{i}u_{1},-\mathrm{i}u_{2})u_{1}^{\varepsilon_{1}}u_{2}^{\varepsilon_{2}},\end{split}

where
s
is defined in (
77
).
